### PLATE-VERBATIM: the-old-man-on-his-knees

*Candidate 2.* Anchor: `images/portraits/severin.png`. Ch. 37 canon: *Behind him his mother took the child out of her coat and put him into the old man's arm, and Severin, on his knees, took him without looking up and put his other hand flat on the wet sand and left it there* …

**ADOPTED — `images/plates/ch37-the-old-man-on-his-knees.png`, first run (author, s58). The block below is the author's optimised version of this sheet's, stored exactly as run.**

**PROVENANCE OF THE FILE:** the adopted image was taken from the session's cached copy of the run — 1024×1536, JPEG-sourced, converted to PNG on the way in. **If the original from the tool is to hand it should overwrite this file at the same path**; nothing else changes if it does.

**THE HAND WON, AGAINST A FACE AND A NEWBORN.** Nearest thing to the viewer, palm down with all five fingers on the ground, sitting dark on a film of water throwing the bleached sky back up — and **the sharpest step from light to dark in the picture is its edge against the wet sand**, exactly where the block sited it, plainly wider in the frame than the child's whole head. `ch28/the-hand-back`'s method, run on a picture that also holds an old man's face and a sleeping baby, and neither takes it off the hand.

**THE LINEN IS IN ITS FOURTH PLATE AND ITS CLEANEST** — the cloth drawn over the crown, the strip level and clear of the nose and the mouth, one fist shut, no eye anywhere on him. **Four plates now, and not one of them has explained it.** The page does not say which arm took the child; the block picked the left and put the right on the sand.

**AND HE IS SPENT WITHOUT BEING FRAIL**, which is what the optimisation bought. The grey came back fully even with the salt through it as scattered light strands and no streak, seam or patch; the coat reads heavy and well made under the soaking; the gaze is down, the brows unmoved, and nothing in it is praying. The four backs and the six spears both rendered unresolved, which is what makes it the minute rather than a picture of an old man holding a baby.

**WHAT DRIFTED, three, and none of them is a correction owed:**

- **The six on the brown came back MOUNTED.** Upright spears as written, but they sit on horses; the page walks six men down a beach. At that size it is a silhouette decision the block never made either way.
- **The ground is a shallow tidal flat and he is kneeling in water**, not on wet sand — the four backs stand in it to the calf. Not what the block said, **and it is what makes the hand work**: the mirror the instrument needed became the whole floor of the picture instead of a puddle.
- **The crop is loose again.** The block cut the bottom edge across the sand just in front of the planted hand with the lower legs out behind him; the picture keeps his folded knee and lower leg. **Twelfth instance, and still never once corrected.**

**A FINISH NOTE, since the folder has not settled finish:** the coat and the skin came back under a dense granular stipple — the most worked surface in the set, well past `ch08`'s controlled pair. Adopted as drawn, and recorded because the next block wanting this level of resolution now has a specimen.

**THE OPTIMISATION IS A LESSON ABOUT THE BLOCK, NOT ABOUT THE PICTURE.** Five changes, and the first is the one that mattered:

1. **§Damage was applied to his coat and not to his body.** Mine wrote the month at sea as pure subtraction — *gone thin, with salt dried pale in his hair and his beard* — with nothing named that survived it. The author's writes it on §AGE's own formula for a grown face: *it is all on the surface: the skin scoured by wind and sun and salt, and the frame under it sound — the strength still there, nothing taken out of him, not frail and not diminished.* **The same rule the next paragraph applied to the coat** (*well made and sound … soaked black*) and missed on the man. `03` has him *not frail, not kindly-grandfatherly*, and one clause is the difference.
2. **The camera is written as what it exposes, not as where the viewer stands** — *the horizon sits level with his shoulders*, against mine's *at about the height of a kneeling man's shoulder*. §Light's rule, pinned inside the frame, and it rendered at that height exactly.
3. **The cut is named against a thing in the picture rather than a landmark on the body** — *the bottom edge runs across the wet sand just in front of his planted right hand … his lower legs and feet are out of it behind him*, against mine's *crosses him at the knees*.
4. **Two clauses were bans and became states.** *No deep shadow anywhere* → *the shadows short and weak and pale everywhere*; *no eye is drawn on the child anywhere* → *no eyes are visible on the child anywhere in the picture*. §Absence's own warning, turned on clauses written as prohibitions.
5. **The salt in the grey got a quantity and a forbidden shape instead of a bare ban** — *dried pale through them as scattered light strands, never as a streak, seam or patch* — which is §Ban the shape, count the thing, and the Vask formula.

```
Image 1: severin.png — half-figure reference of the old man.

Same medium and style as the reference images: loose watercolour and
ink on rough cold-press paper, scratchy ink linework, wash blooms,
bare paper in the lights.

Setting: a flat open beach of wet sand at the edge of a shallow sea,
as loose wash suggestion. Behind him the sand runs up the picture to a
low brown shore, and beyond that a haze that goes back and does not
stop. The light is a high white southern glare out of a bleached sky,
coming down and up again off the wet sand, the shadows short and weak
and pale everywhere in the picture.

Composition: the old man kneels in the near ground, large in the
frame, turned three-quarters toward the viewer, his head bent down
over what he is holding. The viewer is out on the water side and low:
the horizon sits level with his shoulders. The bottom edge of the
picture runs across the wet sand just in front of his planted right
hand, so that both his arms and the child are fully in frame and his
lower legs and feet are out of it behind him.

HIS RIGHT HAND IS FLAT AND OPEN ON THE WET SAND, palm down, all five
fingers spread and every one of them touching the ground, resting
there and carrying no weight. The hand is wider in the frame than the
child's whole head. A thin film of water lies on the sand around it
throwing the bleached sky back up, and the hand sits dark on it.

IN HIS LEFT ARM, HELD AGAINST THE FRONT OF HIS COAT, IS A NEWBORN
CHILD wrapped in a piece of coarse pale cloth, the cloth drawn up over
the crown of his head. From the top of his head to his heel he is
about the length of the old man's forearm. A narrow strip of pale
linen lies level across his eyes, clear of the nose and clear of the
mouth, tucked away behind the ear and neatly done; no eyes are visible
on the child anywhere in the picture. He is asleep, his mouth a little
open, one fist shut against the cloth.

His eyes are down on the child's face, the brows level and unmoved,
the mouth closed inside the beard. He is not praying and he is not
looking at the viewer.

FURTHER UP THE BEACH, BETWEEN HIM AND THE SHORE, FOUR FIGURES STAND
WITH THEIR BACKS TO THE VIEWER, spread in a line, small — each about a
hand tall in the frame — and not resolved: no faces, no features and
no costume detail, only shapes in loose wash, arms down, a blade in
one hand each. Above and beyond them on the brown, smaller again, six
figures come down toward the water with upright spears. Nothing has
happened yet and nobody is touching anybody. There is nothing in the
old man's hands but the child, and no blade anywhere near him.

A month at sea is on him and it is all on the surface: the skin
scoured by wind and sun and salt, and the frame under it sound — the
strength still there, nothing taken out of him, not frail and not
diminished. The hair and beard are fully and evenly grey, a true
neutral grey, and the salt has dried pale through them as scattered
light strands, never as a streak, seam or patch. He wears a plain
heavy coat, well made and sound, open at the throat, soaked black with
water up to the shoulder and salt-stained pale where it has dried, in
cold grey-brown and deep charcoal. No robes, no chain and no ornament
of any kind.

Colour: the sand, the shore and the haze in dust-brown, dull ochre and
pale bone, the sky bleached almost to bare paper, the child's cloth
pale bone and lighter than the sand around it. The sharpest step from
light to dark in the whole picture happens at the edge of his hand
against the wet sand; his face is fully drawn and held at closer,
quieter values with soft wash-bled edges, and the beach, the figures
and the shore stay quiet and soft-edged behind him.

Preserve his face, hair, build and apparent age exactly as shown in
the reference image.
```
